text-fit **9/9**, treatment **14/14**, IMG8 **18/18**, IMG11 **10/10**, and IMG12 **11/11**. The fresh strict snapshot
is **BLOCKED** with provenance **changed=0**, G1 owner approval, G4 live deployment evidence, and

The same continuation also adds an explicit semantic arrangement grammar for the AI client: `slide.arrange:{cols:2..4}`
requests a balanced engine-owned grid without coordinates. A deterministic 2×2 stat-grid proof now passes the layout
invariants (stable digest, two columns/two rows, positive rectangles, and no blocking overlap/off-slide issues); the

It now also exposes `contentSignals` and `decisionAuthority` (`aiOwns`, `engineOwns`, and `sharedReview`) so the
client can distinguish narrative decisions from deterministic geometry, fit, framing, caption-lane, and package work.
The `arrange.cols` contract is now consistent across schema, runtime, and guidance: only **2–4** are accepted;
invalid values produce an actionable warning instead of being silently coerced.
Weighted `arrange.split` now has the same fail-soft discipline: at least two finite positive weights are required;
malformed values produce an actionable warning and never enter rectangle math.
KPI cards now use the shared `value → label → delta` reading order, with a deterministic layout-engine regression
proof; this removes the remaining stat-delta convention defect without any deck-specific patch.
The same verifier now proves effect metadata such as `softEdges` survives dry-run readback, closing that minor
authoring feedback gap while leaving the non-blocking visual-overlap advisory explicit.

The timing contract now also states phase truth: `dryRun` is a script estimate, `resolve` may return measured audio,

**2026-08-03 semantic uploaded-presenter repair continuation:** the public Authoring Excellence API now exposes a

**2026-08-03 native narration-identity continuation:** semantic repair now reasserts the Record Studio narration

**2026-08-03 AI decision-context continuation:** the versioned `authoring-excellence.api.v1` response now includes a
compact `decisionContext` preflight. Before drafting, the AI is told exactly which story, composition, identity, and

**2026-08-03 visible-presenter playback evidence continuation:** the PowerPoint COM playback probe now records and

**2026-08-03 visual-balance decision continuation:** the shared layout engine now returns a versioned
`authoring-excellence.visual-balance.v1` readback on every compiled slide (`slides[].visualBalance`, mirrored under
`appliedDefaults.slides[]`). It reports occupied semantic area, region density, block counts, and a status of
`balanced`, `review`, `imbalanced`, `underfilled`, or `not-applicable`. This gives the AI a measured way to review
left/right or sidebar/main composition and to recognize intentional negative space; it never turns the readback into
author-authored coordinates. The correction rule remains semantic-only: revise layout intent, arrangement, region, or
content meaning and rerun `dryRun`. Focused layout, public semantic, black-box, and TypeScript checks are green.
Zero-density regions remain in the ratio, so an empty side is represented by both the `underfilled` status and its
measured skew rather than being hidden by a non-zero-only calculation.

**2026-08-03 visual-balance schema closure:** the public generation schema now types the same
`authoring-excellence.visual-balance.v1` contract wherever the AI can read the plan: per-slide readback and
`appliedDefaults.slides[]` use one closed schema for mode, status, region density, occupancy, skew, and semantic
correction. This removes an opaque-schema path that could otherwise make a cold client infer the engine contract from

single deck.

**2026-08-03 semantic balance attribution continuation:** each visual-balance region now also reports the contributing

schema marks these identifiers as readback-only.

**2026-08-03 overlap-evidence continuation:** `OBJECT_OVERLAP` now emits an actionable semantic finding with the
conflicting object names, primitive kinds, overlap area/fraction, and whether the collision is text-versus-visual.
This gives the AI a direct correction target—reassign regions, reduce competing blocks, or choose a compatible intent—
without exposing a coordinate-patching route. The layout verifier proves the evidence shape and remains green.

**2026-08-03 text-fit evidence continuation:** `TEXT_OVERFLOW_RISK` now emits measured text length, requested and
resolved font sizes, minimum font size, estimated line count/height, available height, and semantic correction
guidance. The AI can decide whether to shorten/split content, reduce competing blocks, or choose a fit-friendly intent
from engine evidence instead of guessing from an opaque warning; geometry remains engine-owned.

**2026-08-03 off-slide evidence continuation:** `OFF_SLIDE_OBJECT` now emits edge-specific overrun amounts, slide
bounds, tolerance/decorative status, placement source, and semantic correction guidance. This distinguishes intentional
bleed or faithful uploaded geometry from a new authored placement defect while preserving the semantic-only correction
policy.

**2026-08-03 arrangement-balance continuation:** visual-balance readback now recognizes engine-owned `arrange.cols` and

arrangement that does not govern substantive blocks is marked `review` with a semantic correction instead of being
reported as balanced; no coordinate authoring route is introduced.

**2026-08-04 layout-validation diagnostics continuation:** unknown or missing `layoutIntent`, invalid `arrange.cols`, and

**2026-08-04 data-proof diagnostics continuation:** `UNVERIFIED_DATA`, `INVALID_REGION`, and `UNKNOWN_REGION` findings

**2026-08-04 backing-frame overlap continuation:** world depth may add an engine-owned `image-frame` or `media-frame`
backing shape beneath its matching image/video. The layout validator now exempts only that same-block frame/media pair

**2026-08-04 World-margin/recommender parity continuation:** `layoutRecommendations` and `regionCatalog` derive their

was added.

**2026-08-04 ranked-proposal readback continuation:** ranked layout alternatives previously exposed region geometry and
semantic assignment only for the winner. Every candidate now carries its own engine-owned region/assignment readback,
so a cold AI can compare the second and third choices before selecting `slides[].layoutIntent`; authored coordinates
remain forbidden.

**2026-08-04 geometry-form parity continuation:** recommender assignment now uses the same explicit-geometry detector
as compilation for `bbox`, `rect`, `options:{x,y,w,h}`, and top-level `x/y/w/h`. Legacy/private geometry cannot be
misreported as engine placement; public Director coordinate inputs remain fail-closed.

**2026-08-04 semantic-intent / weighted-split parity continuation:** a split intent such as
chart-left-insights-right could previously be pre-gridded by array order when the AI also supplied
arrange split weights, while the recommender still reported chart-left / insight-right. The shared resolver now
keeps semantic intent authoritative, applies the same weighted left/right hosts to recommendations and compilation,
and measures the resolved objects in those hosts. The layout verifier covers proof-first and copy-first ordering, so
AI readback cannot silently disagree with the compiled semantic composition.

**2026-08-04 balance / intent-family readback continuation:** semantic balance now excludes only a matching
engine-owned image/media backing frame from occupied-area density, preventing intentional depth treatment from being
counted as a second proof block. `three-column` text content now resolves through the three semantic hosts instead of
being absorbed into the lead-text stack. Compact intents ignore irrelevant generic `arrange` hints in balance
readback. Regressions cover frame density, three-column text placement, and hero/metric-grid false reviews.

**2026-08-04 current-intent recommender readback continuation:** ranked proposals now retain a valid
`slides[].layoutIntent` explicitly selected by the AI even when the content heuristic would otherwise rank it below
the top alternatives. The current semantic choice remains comparable with two stronger proposals and carries the
same engine-owned regions/assignment readback, preventing a cold client from losing its own decision context.

**2026-08-04 semantic-precedence guidance continuation:** the public `layoutGrammar` now states the precedence rule
explicitly: for split intents, `arrange.split` changes column proportions while the selected intent remains
authoritative for block affinity; content order never overrides the semantic relationship. The same readback rule
documents retention of the current intent in ranked proposals, and the cold semantic verifier guards both statements.

**2026-08-04 proposal assignment / region joinability continuation:** ranked proposal assignments now normalize inset
copy hosts back to their named engine regions (`left`/`right`) instead of returning an internal role such as `copy`
that had no matching geometry entry. Specialized engine slots (metric cards and motion rows) remain explicit semantic
labels. The layout verifier proves image-left and copy-left assignments join to numeric proposal regions, keeping AI
readback directly actionable.

**2026-08-04 status-verifier freshness continuation:** the roadmap consistency gate now checks the live public evidence
counts (**19/19 committed fixtures, 44/44 cold adversarial, 60/60 cold semantic authoring**) instead of accepting the

continuation entries remain intentionally unchanged.

**2026-08-04 proposal-slot geometry continuation:** ranked layout proposals now include `assignmentRects`, an
engine-owned numeric rectangle for every semantic block assignment, including specialized slots such as metric cards,
motion rows, and semantic cards. The semantic assignment label remains stable (`metric-card`, `motion-bar`, etc.), while
the rectangle is exposed only as readback and is never an allowed Director input. The layout, public semantic, and
black-box schema gates cover this contract.

**2026-08-04 semantic-side compiler parity continuation:** sidebar-left/sidebar-right and comparison intents now stay

**2026-08-04 sidebar-callout precedence continuation:** the generic bottom-band callout rule no longer intercepts

**2026-08-04 sidebar principle-API continuation:** `help.capabilities` now explicitly teaches the sidebar semantic

**2026-08-04 all-intent proposal-host parity continuation:** layout recommendations now run the same engine auto-flow

the full body height and readback stays identical between proposal and compilation; the layout verifier asserts the

**2026-08-04 specialized-intent routing continuation:** `title-only`, `section-divider`, and `pull-quote` now bypass
generic array-order pre-gridding and resolve through their specialized engine regions. Proposal and compiled readback
now place title/quote content in the intended title/body hosts (including the centered pull-quote body), with exact
region assertions in the layout verifier.

**2026-08-04 live capability-matrix schema continuation:** generation readback intentionally uses a compact
`capabilityMatrix` for summary detail and the full typed matrix for full detail. The MCP output schema had incorrectly
required full-only fields for both forms, so a cold live client could be rejected before reading the authoring contract.
The schema now accepts and types both forms while preserving the full matrix for `detail:"full"`. The live cold MCP
authoring pipeline passes end-to-end; this is an API transport fix, not a deck-specific workaround.

also performs this local discovery check, so the aggregate release-safety run catches a future local/public contract
split before an AI client can draft against incomplete guidance.

**2026-08-04 world-input contract clarification:** the AI-facing principle API now distinguishes narrative from

corner/fill/border/elevation knobs. This mapping is versioned as `authoring-excellence.world-inputs.v1`, exposed in
both public black-box and local maintainer discovery, and verified without adding a hand-authored geometry path.

with the selected design language instead of encouraging decorative gradients merely to raise a metric.

addressed at the shared Director layer. `authoring-excellence.motion-coverage.v1` is exposed in public

motion is a starting character, that meaningful objects need semantic cues or narration sync, and that decorative
scaffolds must not be animated for score. The cold public semantic-authoring suite passes **62/62**, and a live ESG
MCP + COM smoke run confirms the readback survives dry-run and commit. This improves every World without forcing
gradients or motion into intentionally flat/static compositions.

their final stop even though the quality counter reported gradient surfaces. The shared `gradient()` helper now emits
rounded percentage positions, and the policy integration test checks ordered endpoint coverage. Corrected narrated Aurora
evidence at `outputs/authoring-excellence/lab-feature-launch-aurora-narrated-20260805-gradient-fix-rerun2/` emits
`0/55000/100000`, shows a perceptible diagonal gradient in COM, and passes **MCP + COM 3/3**, design **92**,
  accessibility **100**, quality, narration, and package gates. Fresh Luxe and Candy rotations at
  `outputs/authoring-excellence/lab-coffee-vendor-luxe-20260805-gradient-fix-rerun3/` and
  `outputs/authoring-excellence/lab-quantum-book-club-candy-20260805-gradient-fix-rerun2/` also pass COM 5/5 with
  corrected percentage stops. Flat-world contracts remain unchanged; this is an engine correction applied globally to
gradient-native Worlds.

**2026-08-05 package-level gradient regression guard:** the maintainer lab now runs the read-only
`tools/verify-authoring-excellence-gradient-package.mjs` audit against the saved PPTX and resolved `commit.json`. It
checks every background's OOXML stop range and ordering plus authored `spPr` object fills when
`design-validation.json` is supplied, so a semantic gradient counter cannot hide malformed `0/550/1000` output or a
missing serialized visual surface. The guard intentionally rejects the pre-fix Aurora artifact, passes corrected Aurora at

flat Observatory control at `outputs/authoring-excellence/lab-competitive-analysis-observatory-20260805-gradient-audit-rerun2/`.

passes MCP + COM **5/5**, and its package audit reports **0/0** gradient surfaces.

**2026-08-05 Director gradient-input parity correction:** the shared `gradientPolicyForArtDirection` helper now resolves
the supported `theme.artDirection` compatibility form with the same precedence as the public
`styleIntent.artDirection` form. Before this correction, an inline bespoke `theme.artDirection.surface.fill:"glass"`
could be reported as flat by the standalone policy helper even though the compiler accepted the World. The regression
matrix is now **58/58** (18 Worlds, both explicit modes, bespoke glass/flat, and the compatibility form), and the real
MCP save/package regression is **26/26**, covering all 18 named Worlds, representative light/dark variants, policy
readback, quality readback, `export_spec` World preservation, synthesized flat-vs-gradient background behavior,
empty-styleIntent compatibility, and serialized OOXML stop ordering.

**2026-08-05 bespoke surface synthesis correction:** the same audit found that an inline bespoke World with
`surface.fill:"flat"` was reporting `gradientPolicy.expected=false` while `synthesizeArtDirection` still generated a
gradient canvas. The synthesis layer now derives its canvas fill from the same World contract: glass/lift (or the
gradient-native glass/candy card axes) receive gradients; flat/outline/bare surfaces receive solid canvases. The
58-case policy matrix now also asserts actual background kinds, and the MCP package regression covers both style/theme
bespoke glass and flat cases (**7/7 PASS**). This is a shared Director correction; no deck-specific patch or package
post-processing was used.

**2026-08-05 Director authored-world readback parity correction:** the MCP engine now merges a supported
`theme.artDirection` into persisted `authoredStyleIntent` when `styleIntent` is empty or only partially specified.
This prevents quality validation and export readback from losing the World that the compiler actually used. The cold
MCP package regression now passes **8/8**, including an empty-`styleIntent` + flat-theme case; the change is confined to
the shared engine boundary and does not expose implementation details on the public surface.

**2026-08-05 complete World package-oracle expansion:** the saved-PPTX gradient regression was expanded from
representative samples to **26/26**: all 18 named Director Worlds, four explicit light/dark variants, style/theme
bespoke glass and flat surfaces, and the empty-`styleIntent` compatibility case. Every case checks the policy,
quality readback, `export_spec` preservation, and actual OOXML gradient/solid background result. This closes the
remaining gap between the 58-case plan matrix and serialized artifact evidence.

**2026-08-05 semantic balance decision correction:** moderate `visualBalance.status:"review"` now yields a
`review-semantic-balance` next action rather than the severe `rebalance-semantic-layout` command. Underfilled and
severely imbalanced splits remain actionable rebalances; proof-led compositions with intentional visual dominance are
left for the AI to inspect against story and proof. The public semantic-authoring contract now verifies this distinction
(**65/65 PASS**) without exposing geometry or implementation details.

**2026-08-05 image-provider fail-fast regression:** the offline image-provider contract now explicitly proves that
an HTTP 400 from a Modal image endpoint is not retried against the same endpoint (`verify-image-gen-provider.mjs`,
**68/68 PASS**). Endpoint rotation/failover remains available for a healthy alternate, while request/configuration
errors cannot multiply a long cold-start wait. A fresh parking-pitch Director rerun remained externally blocked by
the configured provider returning HTTP 400 after its long request window; no layout or World defect was inferred and
no deck-specific workaround was introduced.

**2026-08-05 semantic motion-advisory correction:** `LOW_MOTION_COVERAGE` now describes the actual story contract.
Non-narrated modern decks receive semantic guidance for meaningful story objects; narrated decks retain
phrase-anchored narration guidance. The public cold semantic verifier added this black-box regression and now passes
**66/66**; no authored geometry, decorative animation, or deck-specific patch was introduced.

**2026-08-05 quality-verifier front-door correction:** `verify-quality-deterministic-v1.mjs` was still constructing a
deck through forbidden primitive calls, so it failed under the current MCP-only authoring boundary. It now composes a
minimal Director JSON spec through the public dispatcher, validates modern versus narrated outcomes, and disposes the
deck through the same lifecycle; the verifier passes with semantic non-narrated motion guidance (**5 advisory warnings,
2 narration blockers, 2 metadata-leak findings**). `LOW_3D_MOTION_COVERAGE` received the same narration-aware
correction in the shared quality engine; no product deck or low-level package patch was used.

**2026-08-05 explicit Director gradient vocabulary:** the public `authoringPrinciples.gradientPolicy` now exposes
`gradientNativeWorlds:["aurora","luxe","candy"]` and the complementary 15 `intentionallyFlatWorlds`. The same
constants drive World synthesis and quality accounting, preventing the AI from inferring gradient treatment from
labels or adding decorative gradients to flat/outline/quiet Worlds. The cold public semantic gate remains **66/66**;
the World policy matrix remains **58/58**, and the saved-PPTX package regression remains **26/26**.

**2026-08-05 Director verification readback correction:** `validate_quality` now refreshes the nested public Director
status after it records a successful verification stage. A green quality response therefore reports the actual
post-gate state (`readyForSave:true`, verification completed, and `verificationEvidence.ok:true`) instead of the
stale pre-gate status that could still say `readyForSave:false`. The cold MCP quality verifier now asserts this
contract, and the full development release-safety aggregate remains **95 registered / 92 PASS / 3 optional skips /
0 failures**. No save behavior or package post-processing changed; this is an AI-facing engine readback correction.

**2026-08-05 Director semantic-layout alias correction:** a cold seed-pitch replay exposed a shared-engine failure:
the AI's deterministic `comparison-left-proof-right` intent and `rightTwoThirds` proof region were previously treated as
unknown, so the fallback title/body layout placed a full-width metric row over the left explanation. The engine now
publishes and normalizes only explicit compatibility aliases, derives the one-third/two-thirds regions from the same
engine-owned body box, exposes the canonical readback, and rejects unresolved `UNKNOWN_LAYOUT_INTENT`/`UNKNOWN_REGION`
at commit. The original cold fixture now resolves to `comparison`, keeps all proof cards inside the right two-thirds
lane, and has no `OBJECT_OVERLAP`; virtual semantic roles `media` and `caption` remain routed by the selected layout
intent/caption-safe context rather than treated as geometry names. The layout verifier includes this regression. No
deck-specific geometry or package post-processing was introduced. Caption/label-only support lanes now remain an
inspectable `visualBalance.status:"review"` instead of being misclassified as severe imbalance.

**2026-08-05 region-plan fail-closed correction:** malformed semantic region plans no longer silently coerce an unknown
axis to columns or clamp non-positive weights. The Director now reports actionable `REGION_PLAN_INVALID` evidence and
blocks commit until the AI supplies `columns`/`rows`, matching positive weights, and a valid engine-owned area. This
also rejects unusable gaps, duplicate names, and sub-0.01in semantic lanes, keeping region-plan semantics aligned with
the same commit-time safety contract as layout intents and named regions.

**2026-08-05 weighted-arrangement numeric-stability correction:** `arrange.split` now rejects overflow, underflow, and
sub-minimum ratios before region math. The engine no longer emits `NaN`, `Infinity`, or zero-width semantic lanes from
finite-looking weights; it returns actionable `ARRANGE_SPLIT_INVALID` evidence and uses the safe default arrangement.

**2026-08-05 dense-stack safety correction:** a high-cardinality semantic `arrange:"stack"` probe exposed that a
full-width stack could place blocks below the slide instead of giving the AI a semantic correction. The shared layout
engine now detects lanes below the 0.45in minimum, falls back to its bounded three-column grid, and emits
`ARRANGE_STACK_TOO_DENSE` with block count, resolved lane height, and semantic alternatives (reduce content, split the
story, or choose `arrange.cols:2–4`). The regression proves 40 text-shape blocks remain finite, positive, on-canvas, and
free of off-slide/overlap errors. This is an engine/readback correction; no deck patch or authored coordinates are used.

**2026-08-05 high-cardinality split/sidebar continuation:** the same stress corpus exposed two adjacent density paths:
weighted `arrange.split` lanes previously put all extra blocks in the last column (which could yield negative heights),
and `sidebar-left|right` main/rail stacks could exceed the slide bottom after their minimum-height clamp. Extras now
distribute round-robin across weighted lanes; dense splits use a bounded grid and emit `ARRANGE_SPLIT_TOO_DENSE`, while
dense sidebar lanes use an in-lane grid and emit `ARRANGE_SIDEBAR_TOO_DENSE`. Forty-block split and sidebar regressions
now remain finite, positive, on-canvas, and overlap-free, with public semantic correction actions. This is shared engine
behavior, not a deck-specific workaround.

**2026-08-05 dense-column-grid continuation:** a 40-block `arrange.cols` probe then exposed a quieter failure mode:
two- and three-column requests stayed on-canvas but compressed rows below the readable minimum, producing repeated
text-fit warnings without a single summary correction. Dense grids now widen to the bounded four-column engine grid
when possible and emit actionable `ARRANGE_COLS_TOO_DENSE` readback (requested/resolved columns, measured row heights,
and split/reduce-content alternatives). The new 2/3/4-column regression keeps all 40 semantic blocks finite,
positive, on-canvas, and overlap-free; the fix remains engine-level and geometry-free at the public Director boundary.

**2026-08-05 visual-hero lane correction:** replaying the robotics keynote corpus against the current solver exposed a
shared `hero` edge case: a side-pinned callout was compacted correctly, but a model3D hero still used the full body
lane and overlapped the callout at 100% of the callout area. The hero solver now reserves the opposite semantic
two-thirds host for the focal visual (and optional hero copy). A model3D + `leftThird` callout regression proves the
proposal is finite, on-canvas, and overlap-free without authored geometry or opaque-native payloads.

**2026-08-05 Director World data-language hardening:** the shared data renderer now carries the selected World panel
surface into native table bodies and derives World-family borders/rules. Dark Luxe tables resolve to an espresso panel
(`221E17`) with a gold rule instead of the former generic navy fill; poster Worlds retain accent hard edges, while
rule Worlds use an underline-only header unless a cell fill was explicitly authored. Chart contrast now uses the
mode-corrected canvas rather than an obsolete raw palette background. Default shadows also follow `depthPolicy.shadow`:
Editorial, Blueprint, Brutalist, Observatory, Horizon, and Mono no longer receive accidental data/card shadows, while
Luxe/Aurora/Candy retain governed elevation and explicit author shadows still win. The new World data-style matrix
passes all cases, including contradictory bespoke glass palettes and corrected light/dark polarity. No deck-specific
patch or package post-processing was used.

**2026-08-05 semantic hero-region vocabulary correction:** the public `hero` region used by the documented 3D/image
front-door recipes is now a governed virtual semantic region. It routes through the selected layout solver rather than
being treated as an unknown raw rectangle, so `region:"hero"` works consistently on hero and title-body slides while
preserving the no-authored-geometry boundary. The full 18-World narration/build/native-3D save matrix is green.

**2026-08-05 Director data-style capability exposure:** the World-aware chart/table renderer is now explicit in the
AI-facing Authoring Excellence contract as `authoring-excellence.data-style.v1`. `authoringPrinciples.dataStylePolicy`
describes the card-axis-to-data-language mapping (grid, rule, bold-flat, or filled), the semantic inputs and safe
readback; `appliedDefaults.dataStyle` reports the resolved table style, World panel surface, border, and rule color.
This closes the capability gap where the engine already made the correct data-style decision but the AI could not
inspect the rule before correcting a draft. The cold public policy proof, World data-style matrix, gradient policy,
layout engine, table-header, and full release-safety suite remain green; no geometry or package patch was introduced.

**2026-08-05 brand-surface continuity correction:** the `brandKit` front door now preserves a continuous
`surface` object independently of its optional card preset. A semantic brand request such as `card:"bare"` plus
`surface.fill:"glass"` therefore remains gradient-native instead of silently losing the surface override during
normalization. The World treatment regression now covers this combined input through the compiler and verifies the
gradient policy, metric-card surface, and AI-facing readback; explicit styleIntent precedence remains unchanged.

**2026-08-05 palette readback correction:** `paletteHealth.accentOnCanvas.visible` now evaluates the resolved
engine-nudged accent rather than treating any correction as a visibility failure. Low-contrast brand accents remain
author-visible after the shared chart/shape nudge and are reported with an actionable correction note; the World
data-style regression covers the resolved visibility result.

**2026-08-05 theme-ramp visibility correction:** native theme consumers now receive the same resolved accent nudge as
charts and shapes. `themeColorSchemeForSpec` derives accent1–3 against the resolved World panel, so low-contrast brand
colours cannot disappear in SmartArt or scheme-driven series while the authored palette remains visible in readback.
The low-contrast World regression verifies `accent1` parity with the resolved renderer value.

**2026-08-05 gradient stop-input hardening:** the shared Director gradient boundary now accepts both its historical
normalized `0..1` stop inputs and the library's percentage `0..100` contract, clamps non-finite/out-of-range values,
and preserves the existing ordered OOXML output. This prevents a future World or surface addition from accidentally
serializing a percentage twice (for example `55` → `5500`) or emitting malformed positions. The focused World policy,
package regression, layout-engine, TypeScript, and full development release-safety suites remain green; no public
coordinate or package-postprocessing path was introduced.

The boundary now has direct regression cases for normalized, percentage, NaN, infinity, negative, and over-100 inputs;
all cases resolve to finite bounded percentages before the World matrix runs.

### 2026-08-12 — Screenshot-first SmartArt Quick Styles applicability evidence

The SmartArt Design ribbon was inspected visually before automation. Its expanded Quick Styles surface is an owner-drawn PowerPoint popup, so the UI tree exposes the gallery container rather than reliable per-tile names. `tools/capture-powerpoint-smartart-style-tiles-ui.ps1` now captures the observed tile candidates through guarded coordinate selection and COM QuickStyle readback on an isolated copy. The first Numbered Dots Horizontal capture is `outputs/authoring-principle-corpus/smartart-style-ui-capture/20260812_151320/smartart-style-tile-capture.json`: 14 candidates, 14 accepted native styles (five simple/Best Match plus nine 3-D), each with a screenshot and editable PPTX snapshot. This is positive evidence that the gallery variants are real native styles, not repeated images.

The evidence is deliberately scoped to the observed layout. It does not claim every SmartArt layout has 14 tiles; the remaining engine/catalog task is to repeat this screenshot-first capture per supported layout family and record applicability/order, then expose only validated executable IDs to AI recommendation. The coverage verifier records the evidence as `smartartStyleUiCapture` with candidate/accepted counts while keeping the family open for per-layout applicability and release governance.

The same guarded capture was then repeated for `Numbered Dots Vertical` at `outputs/authoring-principle-corpus/smartart-style-ui-capture/20260812_151852/smartart-style-tile-capture.json`; the manifest records the layout ID and 14/14 native style readbacks. This confirms that style IDs can be shared across at least two layouts while preserving an explicit layout applicability key. Distinct families still require their own capture; no global SmartArt tile count is asserted.

The subsequent coverage refresh is `outputs/authoring-principle-coverage/20260812T122003Z/authoring-principle-coverage.json` (`OPEN`); the capture evidence is accepted and release gates are unchanged.

The successful screenshot-first runs are consolidated in `outputs/authoring-principle-corpus/smartart-style-ui-catalog/20260812T122609Z/smartart-style-applicability-catalog.json`: four layout IDs across timeline, hierarchy, list/picture, and process categories; 56 accepted tile states; 14 unique native QuickStyle IDs; and no missing artifacts. This is deliberately partial evidence with explicit layout applicability and tile order, not a global style-count claim. Matrix, cycle, pyramid, relationship, and the remaining Office layout families remain open.

### 2026-08-12 — Expanded SmartArt Quick Style applicability batch

### 2026-08-13 15:20Z — AI Director selection-authority alignment

The umbrella direction is now explicit across the goal, public capability registry, and roadmap: the AI is the creative
and editorial authority and makes the final choice of visual medium, variant, treatment, layout, theme, master, and
motion. The engine exposes the vocabulary, examples, executable IDs, geometry, serialization, safety, and validation
needed to execute that choice. Feature-specific catalog operations are exposure aids only; they must never silently
select, override, or downgrade the AI's design. Only malformed, unexposed, unsafe, or package-invalid requests fail,
and they fail without substitution.

This realignment changes the completion interpretation: exhaustive Ribbon parity is evidence and confidence work, not
permission for the engine to become the designer. A family is AI-authoring-ready when the AI can select from its
truthfully exposed vocabulary and the engine can validate/execute that selection; exact per-tile Office parity remains
a separate evidence closure track.

The primary quality loop is now AI-led sampling: generate varied prompts/specs through MCP, let the AI select the exact
capability, execute it, then inspect PowerPoint readback, render, package, accessibility, and owner review. Any failure
is corrected at the shared engine/catalog layer; the engine does not redesign the request. “Applicability” is retained
only as descriptive catalog metadata for the AI, not as an engine ranking or design gate.

### 2026-08-13 16:13Z — Catalog closure becomes the critical path

The updated umbrella goal makes catalog closure the hard technical milestone. Every known capability must be accounted
for as executable, preserve-only, fallback, or planned; every executable row must have a stable opaque ID, AI-readable
description, schema, example, and exact MCP execution path. The fastest verification path is cold-AI sampling with unseen
prompts, AI-owned selection, MCP execution, and PowerPoint/package/visual/accessibility/black-box review. Sampling proves
the catalog is usable but cannot excuse missing rows. Existing chart/SmartArt/table evidence is reused first; repeated
PowerPoint UI traversal is reserved for a missing/disputed catalog entry or a real sample-exposed execution defect.

### 2026-08-13 16:30Z — Complete existing-capability MVP and continuous catalog train

This checkpoint supersedes the earlier selective “Premium Core” interpretation. MVP is now the complete product surface
that exists when catalog closure is reached, not a hand-picked subset. The cutoff is temporal: catalog first, then freeze
one `mvpBaselineInventory`; every product capability implemented at that point belongs to MVP. Only capabilities with no
implementation at freeze, or first implemented afterwards, remain in continuous progress.

The inventory is generated from source, schemas, MCP and private product surfaces, registries, frozen corpora,
distribution artifacts, and active plans. A capability discovered during closure cannot be deferred to accelerate
launch. If an existing implementation is incomplete, unreachable, unsafe, visually wrong, package-invalid, or awaiting
a required owner approval, that condition blocks MVP until corrected or approved. Preserve-only and fallback behavior
already implemented is included honestly as preserve-only/fallback; it is not misrepresented as native authoring.

Consequently, provider-backed image generation, identity/avatar/voice and Record Studio paths, native-3D presenters,
dedicated interactions, motion/kinetic/Morph capabilities, imported-deck revision, themes/masters, review/collaboration,
and secondary native objects are part of MVP wherever the frozen inventory confirms they are implemented. There is no
private-preview escape hatch for an existing product capability. Maintainer diagnostics, reference builders, UI capture
harnesses, fixtures, and research artifacts remain evidence tools rather than product capabilities, with an explicit
auditable classification.

MVP closure requires complete baseline accounting, opaque IDs and AI-readable schemas/examples, exact MCP
creation/revision reachability without substitution for executable rows, exact behavioral proof for preserve-only and
fallback rows, repair-free package/readback/render/editability/accessibility/black-box evidence, and a risk-weighted
cold-AI portfolio covering every baseline family. The portfolio samples combinations; mechanical tests reach every ID.

Release governance now uses `baselineMvpGate`. It covers every row in `mvpBaselineInventory`, including every applicable
identity, licensing, quality, deployment, and owner-approval gate. It cannot turn green by disabling or relabeling an
existing implementation. The provisional inventory fingerprint is bound to a clean reviewed immutable SHA at release.
After that freeze, genuinely new work follows catalog exposure → exact reachability → focused cold-AI sample →
PowerPoint/package/visual proof → next catalog fingerprint without moving the original MVP finish line.
